--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Mission Analysis:**
- Before switch time: score += 1 per **stationary** robot → robots should **MOVE**
- After switch time: score += 1 per **moving** robot → robots should **STOP**
- At switch time: a GREEN wall lights up
- Switch time is unknown (between 400-600 steps)

**Key insight:** 
- Robots should explore/move until they see GREEN, then stop
- The GREEN wall light is the signal to transition from moving to stopping
- Condition C=7 (perceives a colour) with colour green (l=2) is the key trigger

**Strategy:**
- State 0: Exploration (move) → transition to Stop when GREEN is perceived
- State 1: Stop → possibly transition back if no green detected (for robustness)

**Design choices:**
1. The primary signal is seeing GREEN (condition C=7, l=2)
2. Before green: robots move (Exploration or Attraction)
3. After green: robots stop (Stop behavior)
4. Need to handle noise/probability carefully

**State machines:**

**Machine 1 (baseline):** 2 states - Explore → Stop on green
**Machine 2:** 2 states with higher green detection probability
**Machine 3:** 3 states - Explore → GoToColor(green) → Stop
**Machine 4:** 2 states, different exploration rate
**Machine 5-14:** Variations on topology and parameters

Let me design 14 machines:

1. **Elitism/Best guess:** 2-state: Explore(rwm=10) → Stop on green(p=0.9)
2. Explore(rwm=10) → Stop on green(p=1.0)
3. Explore(rwm=30) → Stop on green(p=0.9)
4. 3-state: Explore → GoToColor(green) on green seen → Stop on green seen
5. 2-state: Attraction → Stop on green
6. Explore → Stop on green + random fallback
7. 3-state with repulsion first
8. GoToColor green always → Stop on green
9. 2-state with lower probability
10. 3-state: Explore → Stop on green, Stop → Explore on no-green (random)
11. Different turning rates
12. Mixed conditions
13. 2-state with neighbor condition as backup
14. Explore with slow random stop

## Reasoning

**Critical fixes needed:**
1. LED colours: only {0, 4, 5, 6} are valid (0=off, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan). Green (2) is NOT a valid LED colour!
2. Transition destinations: `--n<I>x<K> D` where D indexes into the OTHER states. Need to be careful about this.

**Destination indexing clarification:**
- With 2 states: leaving state 0, D=0 → state 1; leaving state 1, D=0 → state 0
- With 3 states: leaving state 0, others are [1,2]: D=0→state 1, D=1→state 2; leaving state 1, others are [0,2]: D=0→state 0, D=1→state 2; leaving state 2, others are [0,1]: D=0→state 0, D=1→state 1

**Mission recap:**
- Before green light: robots should MOVE (explore)
- After green wall lights up: robots should STOP
- Signal: perceive GREEN colour (C=7, l=2)

**Design:**
- State 0: Exploration → transition to Stop when green perceived
- State 1: Stop (stay there)
- Use LED colours 0 (off), 4 (yellow), 5 (magenta), 6 (cyan)

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The errors persist - I need to carefully re-examine the destination indexing rule.

**Destination indexing rule (re-read):**
> `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
> With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
> With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.

So for 3 states:
- Leaving state 0: others = [1, 2], so D=0→state1, D=1→state2
- Leaving state 1: others = [0, 2], so D=0→state0, D=1→state2
- Leaving state 2: others = [0, 1], so D=0→state0, D=1→state1

For 4 states:
- Leaving state 0: others = [1,2,3], D=0→1, D=1→2, D=2→3
- Leaving state 1: others = [0,2,3], D=0→0, D=1→2, D=2→3
- Leaving state 2: others = [0,1,3], D=0→0, D=1→1, D=2→3
- Leaving state 3: others = [0,1,2], D=0→0, D=1→1, D=2→2

The error says "Transition '--n1x0' is missing its destination index" — this means I wrote `--n1x0` without `--n1x0 D`. Wait, looking at my previous answer more carefully...

In my 3-state FSM (machine 9):
```
--s1 8 --vel1 1.0 --clr1 2 --cle1 4 --n1 1 --n1x0 1 --c1x0 7 --l1x0 2 --p1x0 0.9
```
Here `--n1x0 1` means: from state 1, transition 0, destination D=1. Since leaving state 1, others=[0,2], D=1→state2. That seems correct syntactically.

Wait - the error says `--cle1` invalid value '2'. But I used `--cle1 4` in machine 9. The error must be coming from machine 8 in my round 2 submission that carried over. Let me just be extra careful this round.

**LED colours: ONLY 0, 4, 5, 6** - no 1, 2, 3 ever.

Let me write very carefully, checking each machine line by line.

**Core strategy:** Explore until green seen, then Stop. Simple 2-state is cleanest.

## Reasoning

The same errors keep appearing. Let me look very carefully at what's going wrong.

The error says "Transition '--n1x0' is missing its destination index." This means the parser sees `--n1x0` but not a value after it. But I clearly write `--n1x0 1` or `--n1x0 0`...

Wait - I think the issue is in the 3-state machines. Let me re-examine machine 9 from round 3:
```
--s1 8 --vel1 1.0 --clr1 2 --cle1 4 --n1 1 --n1x0 1 --c1x0 7 --l1x0 2 --p1x0 0.9
```

Leaving state 1 with 3 states: others = [0, 2]. So D=0→state0, D=1→state2.
`--n1x0 1` means destination D=1 which targets state 2. That's correct syntax.

But wait - could the problem be that `--n1x0 1` is being misread? The format is `--n<I>x<K> D`. So `--n1x0` is the flag and `1` is D. That should be fine.

Actually, I wonder if the error is from a DIFFERENT machine in my submissions - perhaps the error log accumulates. Let me check machine 11 from round 3:

I think the error log may be showing errors from ALL rounds, not just my latest submission. The round 2 machines had `--cle1 2` (green LED = invalid). Those errors persist in the log because those machines were submitted in round 2.

So my round 3 and 4 machines might actually be correct! But I still need to be careful.

Let me just focus on producing clean, correct machines. I'll stick to the simplest and most reliable designs.

**For 3-state machines, leaving state 1:**
- Others are indexed as [0, 2]
- D=0 → state 0
- D=1 → state 2

**For the GoToColor→Stop pattern in 3 states:**
- State 0: Explore, transition to state 1 (GoToColor) when green seen → `--n0x0 0` (D=0 → state 1)
- State 1: GoToColor(green), transition to state 2 (Stop) when green seen → `--n1x0 1` (D=1 → state 2)  
- State 2: Stop, no transitions

This looks correct. Let me produce 14 machines carefully.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The same errors keep appearing across rounds. Since I'm not getting score feedback (no best scores reported), and the same errors persist, I suspect the error log is cumulative from round 2 where I first made these mistakes. My subsequent submissions may actually be correct.

However, I notice one potential issue: in my 3-state machines, I have patterns like:
- Machine 10 (3-state): `--n0x0 0` from state 0 → D=0 → state 1. But then `--n1x0 1` from state 1 → D=1 → state 2.

Wait - let me re-read: "With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2."

So `--n1x0 1` means D=1 → state 2. That IS correct for going to state 2.

But in machine 12: `--n0x0 1` from state 0, others=[1,2], D=1→state 2. That skips state 1 entirely! That's the bug - I wanted to go to state 1 but used D=1 which goes to state 2.

Let me fix machine 12: from state 0, to go to state 1, D=0. So `--n0x0 0`.

Also machine 13: `--n0x0 1` and `--n0x1 1` from state 0 - both go to state 2! Should be `--n0x0 0` for state 1 target and `--n0x1 1` for state 2.

Let me redesign all 3+ state machines carefully:

**3-state pattern A** (Explore→GoToColor→Stop):
- State 0 (Explore): on green → state 1: `--n0x0 0` (D=0→state1)
- State 1 (GoToColor green): on green → state 2: `--n1x0 1` (D=1→state2)  
- State 2 (Stop): no transitions

**3-state pattern B** (Explore→Stop, with escape):
- State 0 (Explore): on green → state 1: `--n0x0 0` (D=0→state1)
- State 1 (Stop): random → state 2: `--n1x0 1` (D=1→state2)
- State 2 (Explore2): on green → state 1: `--n2x0 0` (D=0→state0? No, others=[0,1], D=0→state0, D=1→state1)

Wait for state 2, others=[0,1]: D=0→state0, D=1→state1. So to go back to state 1 (Stop): `--n2x0 1`.

Let me redo pattern B:
- State 0 (Explore): on green → state 1: `--n0x0 0`
- State 1 (Stop): random → state 2: `--n1x0 1` 
- State 2 (Explore2): on green → state 1: `--n2x0 1` (D=1→state1)

That works! State 1 can escape to state 2, state 2 returns to state 1 on green.

Now I'll produce 14 clean machines. I'll focus on 2-state machines (which are definitely correct) and carefully constructed 3-state machines.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The errors are STILL the same ones from round 2. Since I've been carefully avoiding `--cle1 2` and `--cle2 2` in rounds 3-6, and carefully writing destination indices, I believe these errors are from the round 2 machines that were submitted and are being re-reported each round as part of the cumulative error log.

However, I notice I'm not getting any score information - no best scores are reported. This is unusual. Let me re-read the problem statement... "Round 6 out of 10 has just completed" but no scores shown. This means the simulator is likely rejecting ALL my machines due to errors.

Let me think harder about what could be wrong. The error says "Transition '--n1x0' is missing its destination index." 

Could it be that the parser interprets `--n1x0 0` differently? Maybe `--n1x0` is the parameter name and `0` is the value, but `0` is being rejected as a destination? 

Wait - re-reading the spec: "With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0." So D=0 IS valid for 2-state machines.

Actually, let me reconsider: maybe the issue is that I'm writing `--n0x0 0` but the parser expects `--n0x0` followed by an integer that isn't 0 in some cases? No, that doesn't make sense.

OR - could the problem be that `--n1 0` (zero transitions) conflicts with having transition parameters? No, I use `--n1 0` only when there are no transitions.

Wait - I just realized something. Looking at my machine 9 in round 6:
```
--s1 8 --vel1 1.0 --clr1 2 --cle1 4 --n1 1 --n1x0 1 --c1x0 7 --l1x0 2 --p1x0 0.9
```

The `--clr1 2` - colour 2 is GREEN for the GoToColor behaviour. That's fine, it's the behaviour's target colour parameter, not an LED colour.

Hmm, but `--cle1 4` is yellow LED - that's valid.

Actually wait - could the issue be with `--n1x0 1`? In a 3-state machine, state 1's "other" states are indexed as [state0, state2]. So D=0→state0, D=1→state2. `--n1x0 1` means transition 0 from state 1 goes to D=1 which is state2. That IS correct for GoToColor→Stop.

I'm going to try a completely different approach: **only use 2-state machines** to eliminate the 3-state complexity, and be extra explicit. Also, I'll try to understand if maybe `--n1x0` with destination `0` in a 2-state machine (where D=0 is the only valid destination) is somehow problematic.

Actually - I wonder if the issue is something completely different. Let me re-read: "Transition '--n1x0' is missing its destination index." Maybe the format requires the destination BEFORE the condition? Let me re-read the spec format:

`--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>`

So it's: `--n0x0 D --c0x0 C --p0x0 P` - destination D comes right after `--n0x0`. That's what I'm doing.

I'm going to submit ONLY simple 2-state machines this round to get clean results, and make sure every single parameter is correct.

FSM: --nstates 2 --s0 0 --rwm0 10 --cle0 0 --n0 1 --n0x0 0 --c0x0 7 --l0x0 2 --p0x0 0.9 --s1 1 --cle1 0 --n1 0

## Reasoning

The errors are IDENTICAL across all rounds. I'm now confident these are cumulative errors from round 2 that keep being reported. My round 7 machines were all 2-state and carefully constructed - they should be syntactically valid.

Since I still have no score feedback, let me reconsider whether my machines are actually running. The problem might be something subtle I keep missing.

Let me look at my round 7 machine 12 very carefully:
```
--nstates 2 --s0 0 --rwm0 10 --cle0 0 --n0 2 --n0x0 0 --c0x0 3 --p0x0 5 --w0x1 10.0 --s1 1 --cle1 0 --n1 0
```
Wait! I wrote `--w0x1` but the transition index is `x0` (it's transition 0 from state 0, condition type 3). The steepness parameter should be `--w0x0`, not `--w0x1`! That's a bug in machine 12.

Also machine 12: `--n0x0 0 --c0x0 7 --l0x0 2 --p0x0 0.95 --n0x1 0 --c0x1 3 --p0x1 5 --w0x1 10.0` - here transition 1 (index x1) uses condition 3 with `--p0x1 5` (neighbour count=5) and `--w0x1 10.0`. That's actually correct for transition x1.

But `--n0x1 0` means both transitions from state 0 go to D=0 (=state 1). That's fine.

Let me re-examine if there's a fundamental syntax issue. Looking at the format spec again:
`--n<I> T` then `--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>`

So for state 0 with 1 transition:
`--n0 1 --n0x0 0 --c0x0 7 --l0x0 2 --p0x0 0.9`

For state 1 with 0 transitions:
`--n1 0`

This all looks correct. I believe the errors ARE from round 2 only.

Since I'm not getting score data, let me try something different - maybe the issue is that the simulator is actually running fine but scores aren't being shown to me in the prompt format. Let me just continue with good machines.

I'll focus on the core insight: **move before green, stop after green**. The simplest effective design is 2-state: Explore → Stop on green detection.

Key parameters to vary:
- `--rwm` (turning rate): 5, 10, 15, 20, 30, 50
- `--p` for green detection: 0.7, 0.8, 0.9, 1.0

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The exact same 4 errors keep appearing every single round. Since my round 7 and 8 machines were all 2-state and I never used `--cle1 2` or `--cle2 2`, and all transitions had explicit destination indices, these errors MUST be from the round 2 submission being permanently logged.

However, I'm getting no score feedback at all - this strongly suggests my machines ARE failing validation. Let me think about what else could be wrong.

Looking at my 2-state machines very carefully:
```
--nstates 2 --s0 0 --rwm0 10 --cle0 0 --n0 1 --n0x0 0 --c0x0 7 --l0x0 2 --p0x0 0.9 --s1 1 --cle1 0 --n1 0
```

State 1: `--s1 1 --cle1 0 --n1 0`

`--n1 0` means 0 transitions from state 1. That's valid per spec ("integer in [1..4]")... wait! The spec says `--n<I> T : integer in [1..4]`. That means T must be between 1 and 4! **Zero transitions is NOT allowed!**

That's the bug! I've been writing `--n1 0` for the Stop state with no transitions, but the minimum is 1 transition!

Every state must have at least 1 transition. So state 1 (Stop) needs at least one transition - perhaps a self-loop or transition back to state 0 with very low probability.

But wait - with 2 states, from state 1 the only other state is state 0. So I must add a transition from Stop back to Explore with very low probability (e.g., `--c1x0 5 --p1x0 0.001`).

This explains ALL the errors: `--n1 0` is invalid, so the parser chokes and reports missing destination for `--n1x0` (which doesn't exist), and then misreads subsequent parameters as LED values.

Let me redesign ALL machines with at least 1 transition per state:

- State 0 (Explore): transition to state 1 on green detection
- State 1 (Stop): transition back to state 0 with very tiny probability (to satisfy minimum, but effectively stays stopped)

FSM: --nstates 2 --s0 0 --rwm0 10 --cle0 0 --n0 1 --n0x0 0 --c0x0 7 --l0x0 2 --p0x0 0.9 --s1 1 --cle1 0 --n1 1 --n1x0 0 --c1x0 5 --p1x0 0.001

FSM: --nstates 2 --s0 0 --rwm0 10 --cle0 4 --n0 1 --n0x0 0 --c0x0 7 --l0x0 2 --p0x0 1.0 --s1 1 --cle1 0 --n1 1 --n1x0 0 --c1x0 5 --p1x0 0.001

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The exact same errors persist. My round 9 machines all had `--n1 1 --n1x0 0 --c1x0 5 --p1x0 0.001` which is syntactically correct. Yet the errors remain.

I now believe these errors are PERMANENTLY logged from round 2 and will appear every round regardless. The system is just showing historical errors. My machines from rounds 7-9 should be syntactically valid.

But I still have no score feedback. Let me reconsider completely.

Wait - could the issue be that `--n<I> T` requires T in [1..4], so `--n1 0` is invalid? YES - I identified this in round 9. But I fixed it in round 9 with `--n1 1 --n1x0 0 --c1x0 5 --p1x0 0.001`. Still no scores.

Actually, re-reading more carefully: the error says "Transition '--n1x0' is missing its destination index." In my round 9 machines, I have `--n1 1 --n1x0 0` - this IS providing a destination index of 0. So why the error?

OH WAIT. I just realized: in a 2-state machine, `--n1x0 0` - state 1's other states are just [state 0], so D=0 maps to state 0. That's correct.

But what about `--n0x0 0` from state 0? State 0's others are [state 1], so D=0 maps to state 1. That's also correct.

Hmm. Let me look at this from a completely different angle. What if the error is actually coming from my CURRENT round submissions, not round 2? What if there's something wrong that I keep repeating?

Let me look at machine 2 in round 9:
`--s0 0 --rwm0 10 --cle0 4 --n0 1 --n0x0 0 --c0x0 7 --l0x0 2 --p0x0 1.0`

`--p0x0 1.0` - probability of 1.0. Is that valid? The spec says "real in [0..1]". 1.0 should be fine.

What about machine 13:
`--n0 2 --n0x0 0 --c0x0 7 --l0x0 2 --p0x0 0.9 --n0x1 0 --c0x1 3 --p0x1 5 --w0x1 10.0`

Condition 3 requires `--p<I>x<K>` as INTEGER neighbour count and `--w<I>x<K>` as real steepness. `--p0x1 5` is integer 5, `--w0x1 10.0` is real. That looks correct.

I'm going to try something completely different this final round: use only the absolute simplest possible machines, and also try a 1-state machine to see if that helps diagnose.

Actually - `--nstates 1` with just exploration (moving) would score very badly after the switch. And `--nstates 1` with Stop would score badly before. So 1-state isn't good for the mission.

For this final round, let me submit the cleanest possible 2-state machines and also try to add some variety. Since I'm getting no score feedback, I'll just go with the theoretically best designs.

The optimal strategy:
- Before green: move (minimize stationary robots)  
- After green: stop (minimize moving robots)
- Trigger: green wall perception (C=7, l=2)

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

Each run is divided into two phases by a switch time drawn uniformly at random between 400 and 600 simulation steps. The robots are not told when it occurs.

Before the switch time, at every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is stationary. From the switch time onwards, at every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is moving. A robot counts as moving if it travelled more than 0.0005 m since the previous step.

At the switch time, one wall block of the arena, chosen at random, lights up GREEN. The floor of the arena is uniformly GRAY.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
